   - Edge (X, F) appears in all paths starting from X.
   - Edge (J, P) connects one of the main branches.
   - Edge (I, O) connects another main branch.

    - Without edge (I, O), the alternative path through I to C is severed.

Therefore, removing edges (X, F), (J, P), and (I, O) ensures that no path exists between X and C.

--- Model Response ---
The two black nodes are labeled F and J. I will perform a breadth-first search (BFS) starting from node F to find the shortest path to node J.

**Breadth-First Search Step-by-Step:**

- **Level 1 (starting from F):**
  - F is connected to E, K, G, H, and M.
  - None of these nodes (E, K, G, H, M) are directly connected to J.

- **Level 2 (from E, K, G, H, M):**
  - From E: E is connected to B and L.
    - B is connected to N, but N is connected to Y, O and nothing else connected to J.
    - L is not connected to any nodes that lead to J.
  - From K: K is connected to G and X.
    - G is already visited.
    - X is connected to nothing directly connected to J.
  - From G: G is connected to E, K, F, and H (all already visited).
  - From H: H is connected to B, G, F (all already visited).
  - From M: M is connected to E and F (all already visited).

- **Level 3 (from B):**
  - B is connected to N and H (already visited).
    - N is connected to C, Y, and O.
    - C is connected to O, I, and P.
      - I is connected to P and J.
      - Checking P does not lead to J.

- **Level 4 (from C):**
  - C is connected to N (visited), O (visited), and I.

- **Level 5 (from I):**
  - I is connected to P and J (reached target node).

Therefore, the path is F -> M -> E -> B -> N -> C -> I -> J.

**Final Answer: Length: 7, Path: [F, M, E, B, N, C, I, J]**

   - Node I is connected to: H, P, and J.

3. **Identify Paths Between G and I**:
   - Path 1: G → H → I
   - Path 2: G → H → P → J → I
   - Path 3: G → E → M → F → X → K → J → P → I
   - Path 4: G → B → C → O → P → J → I

   - The direct path G → H → I can be cut by removing (H, I).
   - The path G → H → P → J → I can be cut by removing (P, J).
   - The longer paths involving M and E, or B, C, and O can be disconnected by ensuring that we have blocked all necessary cross-edges that these paths might use including (H, P).

   - Removing (H, I) disconnects the direct path.
   - Removing (P, J) disconnects the path through P and J.
   - By removing (H, P) we prevent all ways in which paths involving H can navigate through P.
   - By removing (G, H) we isolate G from any path that begins through H.

6. **Final Cut Set**:
   - To ensure all paths are cut efficiently with the minimal number of edges removed, the necessary edges to be removed are (H, I), (P, J), (H, P), and (G, H).

### Final Answer:

2. **Trace all paths between E and I:**
   - Path 1: E → G → H → I
   - Path 2: E → G → H → C → O → P → I
   - Path 3: E → G → H → C → Y → B → N → C → O → P → I
   - Path 4: E → F → X → K → J → P → I

   - **Path 1 (E → G → H → I):**
     - Removing G, H or I would break this path.
   - **Path 2 (E → G → H → C → O → P → I):**
     - Removing G, H, C, O, or P would break this path.
   - **Path 3 (E → G → H → C → Y → B → N → C → O → P → I):**
     - Removing G, H, C, Y, B, N, O, or P would break this path.
   - **Path 4 (E → F → X → K → J → P → I):**
     - Removing F, X, K, J, or P would break this path.

4. **Determine minimal cut set:**
   - From paths 1 and 4, removing both G and P isolates these paths. Note that removing only one of G or P isn't sufficient to disconnect all paths.
   - Removing G and P will ensure that no path remains between E and I through any of the identified paths.

5. **Verify the cut set:**
   - Removing nodes G and P disconnects all paths:
     - Path 1 is broken by removing G or P.
     - Path 2 is broken by removing H, or P
     - Path 3 is broken by removing some combination of G, H, C, Y, B, N, O, P
     - Path 4 is broken by removing F, X, K, J, or P.

Since G and P are the minimal set of nodes required for full disconnection and other combinations do not fully disconnect E and I, we conclude with:

### Step-by-Step Reasoning

1. **Identify the Target Nodes**:
   The two black nodes in the graph are labeled **H** and **N**.

2. **Identify All Paths Between H and N**:
   - Path 1: H → B → C → N
   - Path 2: H → E → M → L → J → I → C → N
   - Path 3: H → E → M → L → J → P → O → N
   - Path 4: H → I → C → N
   - Path 5: H → I → C → O → N

3. **Determine Critical Nodes**:
   To ensure complete disconnection between H and N, any path between them must be severed.

   - Paths 1, 4, and 5 all pass through node **C**.
   - Path 2 and 3 do not traverse node **C**, thus removing **C** alone will not suffice.
   - Paths 2 and 3 pass through node **I** and several other nodes, creating two subsets of paths.
   - Common nodes in these paths are **E** and **O**.
   - Removing **E** would break both paths 2 and 3.

4. **Identify Minimum Cut Set**:
   - Removing node **C** impacts paths 1, 4, and 5.
   - Removing node **E** impacts paths 2 and 3.
   - Thus, removing nodes **C** and **E** guarantees that all paths between H and N are severed.

### Verify the Solution:
- Removing C and E ensures no path exists between the nodes H and N:
  - Removing **C** impacts paths: H → B → C → N, H → I → C → N, H → I → C → O → N
  - Removing **E** impacts paths: H → E → M → L → J → I → C → N, H → E → M → L → J → P → O → N

Thus, by removing both **C** and **E**, all possible paths between H and N are severed.

### Final Answer:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [C, E]

   - Check if any of these nodes are connected to J:
     - Is G directly connected to J? No.
     - Is F directly connected to J? No.
     - Is X directly connected to J? No.

2. **Level 2 (Neighbors of G, F, and X):**
   - Neighbors of G: G is connected to H, F, X, and K.
   - Neighbors of F: F is connected to G, X, M, and E.
   - Neighbors of X: X is connected to F, G, and K.
   - Check if any of these nodes are connected to J:
     - Is H directly connected to J? No.
     - Is M directly connected to J? No.
     - Is E directly connected to J? No.
     - Is L directly connected to J? No.

3. **Level 3 (Continuing from neighbors at Level 2):**

   - Neighbors of M: M is connected to F.
   - Neighbors of E: E is connected to B, F, L, and N.
   - Check if any of these nodes are connected to J:
     - No new connections to J at level 3.

4. **Level 4 (Continuing from neighbors):**

   - Neighbors of N: N is connected to Y, B, E, O, and C.
   - Neighbors of C: C is connected to O, N, P, J, and I.
   - Neighbors of P: P is connected to D, C, I, and J.
   - Check if any of these nodes are connected to J:
     - C is directly connected to J.
     - P is directly connected to J.
     - Extracting paths through B, N, and C or neighboring nodes:
     - From N to C and C to J:
       - Path: K → G → H → B → C → J
     - From N or P directly to J:
       - Path: K → G → H → B → N → C → J

After checking all possible paths, the shortest path from K to J is through node N, C, via an intermediary:
     - Path: K → G → H → B → N → C → J  or
     - Path: K → G → H → B → P → J

After double-checking, the minimum number of steps is 5. The correct path is K → G → H → B → P → J

**Final Answer:** Length: 5, Path: [K, G, H, B, P, J]

2. **Trace all paths between L and J**:
   - Path 1: L -> E -> G -> H -> I -> P -> J
   - Path 2: L -> E -> G -> H -> I -> O -> J
   - Path 3: L -> E -> G -> C -> O -> J
   - Path 4: L -> E -> B -> N -> Y -> O -> J
   - Path 5: L -> E -> G -> H -> I -> P -> D -> O -> J

   - Paths 1, 2, and 5 all pass through node I.
   - Path 3 passes through node C.
   - Path 4 passes through node O.

4. **Identify critical nodes**:
   - Removing node I would break paths 1, 2, and 5.
   - Removing node C would break path 3.
   - Removing node O would break paths 2, 3, and 4.

   To ensure complete disconnection, we need to remove nodes that cover all paths. The minimum set of nodes to remove includes node I and one of the nodes C or O.

5. **Verify the cut set**:
   - Removing nodes I and C will break all paths since node C covers the only path not covered by node I.
   - Alternatively, removing nodes I and O will also break all paths since node O covers the only path not covered by node I.

Since either combination ensures disconnection and both combinations have the same number of nodes (2), the minimal cut set can be either [I, C] or [I, O].

Final Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [I, C] or [I, O].

   - From E: E → P, E → L, E → M, E → J
   - From P: P → J, P → I
   - From I: I → H, I → G, I → F
   - From H: H → K, H → G
5. **Level 4**: Explore the neighbors of the new nodes from Level 3:
   - From L: No new connections that haven't been explored yet.
   - From M: No new connections that haven't been explored yet.
   - From J: J → D
   - From G: G → K, G → F
   - From F: F → K, F → X
   - From K: K → X
6. **Level 5**: Explore the neighbors of the new nodes from Level 4:
   - From D: No new connections that haven't been explored yet.
   - From F: K → X
   - From K: K → X

By examining the connections, the shortest path from N to X is:
- N → C → I → G → F → X

Let’s verify each edge:
- Is node N directly connected to node C? Yes.
- Is node C directly connected to node I? Yes.
- Is node I directly connected to node G? Yes.
- Is node G directly connected to node F? Yes.
- Is node F directly connected to node X? Yes.

Image name: SEIS_task3_gt3_PAIR3
Task: task3_min_edges_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 6
--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed in order to completely disconnect the black nodes E and H, we need to systematically analyze the graph and identify all possible paths between the two nodes. Here’s the step-by-step reasoning:

### Identifying All Paths
1. **Direct Connection:**
   - There is a direct edge (G, H).

2. **Paths through Node G:**
   - E -> L -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> C -> O -> P -> J -> I -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> C -> O -> P -> J -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> C -> O -> P -> J -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> C -> O -> P -> J -> I -> G -> H

3. **Paths through Node Y:**
   - E -> B -> N -> Y -> C -> P -> J -> I -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> Y -> C -> P -> J -> G -> H

4. **Paths through Node M:**
   - E -> L -> M -> F -> X -> G -> H
   - E -> L -> M -> F -> K -> X -> G -> H
   - E -> L -> M -> F -> X -> K -> G -> H

### Minimal Cut Set
To ensure complete disconnection, we must remove at least one edge from every identified path that connects E and H.

1. **Remove (G, H)**: This directly removes the direct path between G and H.

2. **Remove (E, L)**: This removes paths that go through L.
   - Removes: E -> L -> G -> H
   - E -> L -> M -> F -> X -> G -> H
   - E -> L -> M -> F -> K -> X -> G -> H
   - E -> L -> M -> F -> X -> K -> G -> H

3. **Remove (G, C)**: This removes paths that go through G.
   - Removes: E -> B -> G -> H

4. **Remove (J, I)**: This removes paths that go through J.
   - Removes: E -> B -> C -> O -> P -> J -> I -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> C -> O -> P -> J -> I -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> Y -> C -> P -> J -> I -> G -> H

5. **Remove (J, G)**: This removes paths that go through J.
   - Removes: E -> B -> C -> O -> P -> J -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> C -> O -> P -> J -> G -> H
   - E -> B -> N -> Y -> C -> P -> J -> G -> H

The minimal cut set of edges that ensures complete disconnection is:
- (G, H)
- (E, L)
- (G, C)
- (J, I)
- (J, G)

However, the minimal cut set can be reduced by identifying the critical edges:
- (E, L) is sufficient to remove paths through L.
- (G, H) is sufficient to remove direct and indirect paths through G.
- (J, I) or (J, G) is sufficient to remove paths through J.

Thus, the minimal cut set is:
### Final Answer
Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(E, L), (G, H), (J, I) or (J, G)]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 3, 'cut_set': [['H', 'B'], ['H', 'I'], ['E', 'F']]}

Image name: FD-K_task2_gt3_PAIR3
Task: task2_min_nodes_removal
Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b
Mode: few_shot, COT: cot, Attempt: 6
--- Model Response ---
Step-by-step reasoning:

1. **Identify the target nodes**: The two black nodes are H and O.
2. **Trace all paths between H and O**:
   - Path 1: H -> B -> C -> N -> Y -> O
   - Path 2: H -> B -> C -> N -> P -> O
   - Path 3: H -> E -> M -> L -> I -> P -> O
   - Path 4: H -> E -> M -> L -> J -> P -> O
   - Path 5: H -> E -> M -> L -> I -> J -> P -> O
3. **Determine critical nodes**:
   - Path 1 and Path 2 both pass through node C.
   - Path 3, Path 4, and Path 5 all pass through node M.
   - To disconnect H and O, we need to remove at least one node from each critical set of paths.

4. **Analyze minimal cut set**:
   - Removing node C blocks Path 1 and Path 2.
   - Removing node E blocks path that connects to node M for Path 3, Path 4, and Path 5.
   - Removing node M blocks Path 3, Path 4, and Path 5.

5. **Verify necessary set**: Removing nodes C and E ensures that all paths between H and O are broken. This is the minimal cut set because removing any one of these nodes alone would leave at least one path intact.

Final Answer: Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [C, E]
--- Ground Truth ---
